27Coupon rate vs current yield vs YTMCoupon ÷ face / coupon ÷ priceIRR of all cash flowsBase and completeness
28Macaulay vs modified durationWeighted average time (years)% price sensitivity per 1% yield changeTime measure vs sensitivity measure
29Digital (dematerialised) vs tokenized bondElectronic record at a central depositoryOwnership as tokens on a shared ledger; programmableRecord-keeping architecture
30Blockchain vs DLTHash-linked blocksAny shared synchronised ledgerEvery blockchain is a DLT, not vice versa
31Public vs permissioned blockchainOpen to anyoneOnly approved participantsAccess and governance
32Second-party opinion vs verificationPre-issuance framework assessmentPost-issuance check of allocation/KPI dataTiming and object
33Equity vs debt sustainable instrumentsESG funds, impact equityGreen/social/SLB bonds, green loansOwnership vs lending
34Blended finance vs concessional financeStructure combining public/philanthropic and private moneyBelow-market-rate money itselfStructure vs component

Common mistakes and examiner traps (18)

  1. Calling SLB proceeds "for green projects". SLB proceeds are general-purpose; the coupon is linked to KPIs.
  2. Putting purchased electricity in Scope 1. It is Scope 2. Owned generators and company vehicles are Scope 1.
  3. Forgetting unit conversion. kg ÷ 1,000 = tonnes; bps ÷ 10,000 = decimal (25 bps = 0.0025).
  4. Using the SLB step-up for all years. It applies only from the coupon after the observation date (e.g. years 4–5), not from year 1.
  5. Treating CSR and ESG as the same. CSR is an activity/spend; ESG is an assessment framework for risk and performance.
  6. Saying Kyoto bound all countries. Only developed (Annex I) countries had binding targets.
  7. Mixing up COPs. COP26 Glasgow 2021 (1.5°C, coal phase-down, India 2070); COP27 Egypt 2022 (Loss and Damage Fund); COP28 Dubai 2023 (Global Stocktake, transition away from fossil fuels, triple renewables).
  8. ESCert unit. 1 ESCert = 1 tonne of oil equivalent, not 1 tCO2.
  9. PCAF denominator. Use EVIC (or equity + debt), never revenue, for the attribution factor.
  10. WACI weights. Weights are portfolio weights (investment ÷ total), not company emissions shares.
  11. Assuming green bonds are safer. A green bond carries the issuer's credit risk, same as its conventional bonds (except special structures such as project bonds).
  12. Claiming a greenium always exists. It is small and not guaranteed; depends on market and issuer.
  13. Writing "ESG rating = credit rating". ESG ratings assess ESG risk/performance; credit ratings assess default risk (though ESG is now considered in credit ratings).
  14. Premium/discount reversal. Coupon > YTM → premium; coupon < YTM → discount.
  15. Duration sign. Price falls when yields rise: %ΔP ≈ −MD × Δy. Convexity term is always added (positive).
  16. "Carbon neutral" = zero emissions. It means net zero after offsets; gross emissions still exist (Infosys and ITC cases).
  17. Tokenization changes credit risk. It does not; it changes technology, legal and operational risk.
  18. Answering a case without a recommendation. Section C marks require a clear recommendation with conditions and a conclusion.

Glossary A–Z (99 terms)

TermMeaning
AdaptationActions that reduce vulnerability to climate impacts.
AdditionalityA project or reduction happens only because of the finance/credit; without it, it would not occur.
Allocation reportGreen bond report showing how proceeds were spent by project/category.
Attribution factor (AF)Share of a company's emissions assigned to an investor/lender (outstanding ÷ EVIC).
BaselineReference-year value of a KPI against which targets are measured.
Best-in-classSelecting ESG leaders within each sector.
Blended financeUsing concessional capital to mobilise private investment for development.
BlockchainDistributed ledger of hash-linked blocks validated by consensus.
Blue bondUse-of-proceeds bond for ocean and water projects.
BRSRBusiness Responsibility and Sustainability Report — SEBI-mandated ESG disclosure for top 1,000 listed companies.
BRSR CoreSubset of BRSR KPIs requiring reasonable assurance.
Carbon creditTradable certificate representing 1 tCO2e reduced or removed.
Carbon neutralNet zero CO2 after reductions and offsets.
Carbon positiveRemoving/sequestering more CO2 than emitted (ITC's claim).
CBAMEU Carbon Border Adjustment Mechanism charging embedded emissions in imports such as steel and aluminium.
CBDCCentral bank digital currency; India's e₹ (retail and wholesale).
CBDRCommon but Differentiated Responsibilities (UNFCCC principle).
CCTSIndia's Carbon Credit Trading Scheme (2023).
CDMClean Development Mechanism under Kyoto; credits (CERs) from projects in developing countries.
CDPGlobal environmental disclosure system scoring A to D− (F = no disclosure).
Climate financeFinance for mitigation and adaptation.
Climate VaRPotential loss in value under climate scenarios.
CO2eCarbon dioxide equivalent, combining gases by GWP.
ConvexityCurvature of the price–yield relationship; corrects duration estimates.
COPConference of the Parties to the UNFCCC (annual).
Coupon step-upIncrease in an SLB's coupon if the SPT is missed.
CSRCorporate social responsibility; mandatory 2% spend under Sec. 135 for qualifying companies.
CUFCapacity utilisation factor of a power plant.
Current yieldAnnual coupon ÷ current price.
Digital bondBond issued, recorded and settled on digital (usually DLT) infrastructure.
DLTDistributed ledger technology: shared synchronised database without central administrator.
Double materialityConsidering both financial impact on the firm and the firm's impact on society/environment.
Duration (Macaulay)Weighted average time to receive a bond's cash flows.
DvPDelivery versus payment: securities and cash exchanged simultaneously.
Emission factorEmissions per unit of activity (e.g. tCO2/MWh).
EngagementInvestors using dialogue and voting to influence companies.
Equator PrinciplesRisk-management framework for E&S risks in project finance (2003).
ESCertEnergy Saving Certificate under PAT; 1 = 1 toe.
ESGEnvironmental, Social and Governance factors.
ESMSEnvironmental and Social Management System used by lenders.
EVICEnterprise value including cash (market cap + debt, including cash).
Exclusionary screeningExcluding sectors or companies (tobacco, weapons, coal).
Expected lossPD × LGD × EAD.
Financed emissionsEmissions attributed to a lender/investor's financing (Scope 3 cat. 15).
FPICFree, prior and informed consent of indigenous communities.
GBPICMA Green Bond Principles (2014; four core components).
GHG ProtocolGlobal standard for corporate GHG accounting (Scopes 1, 2, 3).
Global StocktakeFive-yearly review of collective progress under Paris; first at COP28.
Green bondBond whose proceeds finance eligible green projects.
Green depositBank deposit whose funds are allocated to green activities (RBI framework, 2023).
Green loanLoan for green projects under the Green Loan Principles.
GreeniumYield advantage of green over comparable conventional bonds.
GreenwashingMisleading or unsubstantiated sustainability claims.
GRIGlobal Reporting Initiative standards for impact reporting.
GWPGlobal warming potential of a gas relative to CO2.
Impact investingInvesting with intention for measurable positive impact plus return.
Integrated reporting (<IR>)Single report combining financial and non-financial value creation (six capitals).
Internal carbon priceShadow price on emissions used in company decisions.
ISSBInternational Sustainability Standards Board (IFRS S1, S2; 2023).
KPIKey performance indicator used to measure sustainability performance.
Loss and Damage FundFund for vulnerable countries hit by climate impacts (agreed COP27).
LTIFRLost-time injury frequency rate per million hours worked.
MaterialitySignificance of an issue to the company's value or impacts.
MicrofinanceSmall-value financial services for low-income households.
MitigationReducing or removing GHG emissions.
Modified durationMacaulay duration ÷ (1 + y); % price change per 1% yield change.
NAPCCNational Action Plan on Climate Change (2008), eight missions.
NDCNationally Determined Contribution under Paris.
Net zeroBalance between emissions produced and removed.
NGFSNetwork for Greening the Financial System (central banks; RBI member since 2021).
NGRBCNational Guidelines on Responsible Business Conduct (nine principles; basis of BRSR).
OracleData feed supplying off-chain information to a smart contract.
PATPerform, Achieve and Trade scheme for industrial energy efficiency.
PCAFPartnership for Carbon Accounting Financials.
PRIUN-supported Principles for Responsible Investment (2006; six principles).
PV01Change in value for a 1 basis point change in yield.
RefinancingUsing green bond proceeds for projects already funded/completed.
Scenario analysisAssessing outcomes under alternative plausible futures.
Scope 1 / 2 / 3Direct / purchased energy / other value-chain emissions.
SDGs17 UN Sustainable Development Goals, 169 targets, for 2030.
Second-party opinion (SPO)Independent pre-issuance review of a sustainable finance framework.
SLBSustainability-linked bond: coupon linked to issuer's KPI performance.
SLLSustainability-linked loan: margin linked to KPIs.
Smart contractSelf-executing code on a blockchain.
Social bondBond financing social projects for target populations.
SPTSustainability performance target on a KPI.
Stakeholder theoryFirms should create value for all stakeholders (Freeman).
Stranded assetAsset that loses value prematurely due to transition (e.g. coal plant).
Stress testingMeasuring losses under severe shocks.
Sustainability bondBond financing a mix of green and social projects.
TaxonomyClassification system defining which activities are green/sustainable.
TCFDTask Force on Climate-related Financial Disclosures (four pillars).
TokenizationRepresenting asset rights as digital tokens on a ledger.
Transition bondBond financing a high-emitter's shift to lower carbon.
Triple Bottom LinePeople, Planet, Profit (Elkington, 1994).
UNFCCCUN Framework Convention on Climate Change (1992).
Use of proceedsRequirement that bond money funds specified eligible projects.
WACIWeighted average carbon intensity of a portfolio.
YTMYield to maturity: IRR of a bond's cash flows at its price.

One-page cheat sheet

Unit 1 — Foundations

  • SF = finance considering ESG + profit for long-term growth.
  • Timeline: 1713 Carlowitz · 1962 Silent Spring · 1972 Stockholm · 1987 Brundtland · 1992 Rio/UNFCCC · 1997 Kyoto · 2004 Who Cares Wins (ESG) · 2006 PRI · 2015 SDGs + Paris.
  • TBL: People, Planet, Profit (Elkington 1994).
  • Scopes: 1 direct, 2 purchased energy, 3 value chain (15 cats). Emissions = activity × EF.
  • ESG risk cycle: identify → assess → mitigate → monitor.
  • Reporting: BRSR (top 1,000; 9 NGRBC principles) + BRSR Core (assurance); GRI impact; SASB/ISSB financial; TCFD 4 pillars.
  • PRI 6 principles; CDP A–D−, F.

Unit 2 — Climate

  • Drivers: fossil fuels, deforestation, agriculture, industry.
  • Paris: well below 2°C, 1.5°C; NDCs; 5-yearly; finance.
  • COP26 Glasgow 2021 (India 2070 net zero) · COP27 Egypt 2022 (Loss & Damage Fund) · COP28 Dubai 2023 (Global Stocktake; triple RE; transition away from fossil fuels).
  • India: NAPCC 8 missions; EPA 1986; NGT 2010; EC Act 2001/2022; PAT (ESCert = 1 toe); CCTS 2023; Green H2; Solar Mission; FAME; BRSR.
  • Testing: scenario (futures) vs stress (shocks); EL = PD × LGD × EAD.
  • CSR = 2% avg NP of 3 years.

Unit 3 — Products

  • Use-of-proceeds: green (GBP 4 components), social, sustainability, blue, transition.
  • Performance-based: SLB (5 components), SLL.
  • Step-up cost = face × bps × years after observation.
  • Impact investing: intentionality, measurability, return (SROI).
  • Carbon trading: cap-and-trade vs baseline-and-credit; tax fixes price, ETS fixes quantity.
  • Green microfinance: solar lamps, cookstoves, resilient farming; EMI formula.
  • Blended finance: first-loss, guarantees. AI/ML: NLP, satellites, estimation.

Unit 4 — Risk and reporting

  • ESG → market, credit, operational, liquidity, reputational risk.
  • PCAF: AF = outstanding ÷ EVIC; FE = Σ AF × E; data quality 1–5.
  • WACI = Σ w × E/Rev. Heat map = L × I.
  • Frameworks: GRI, SASB, TCFD, ISSB S1/S2, CDP, <IR>, BRSR, CSRD (double materiality).
  • Greenwashing controls: SPO, verification, SEBI green debt rules, ESG fund rules, BRSR Core.
  • Future: AI risk tools, blockchain traceability, ISSB, TNFD, transition plans.

Unit 5 — Topics

  • SLB: KPI (material, measurable, verifiable, benchmarkable) → SPT → observation date → step-up.
  • Green bonds: GBP, eligible categories, allocation + impact reports, SDG 7/9/11/12/13.
  • Digital/tokenized bonds: DLT record, atomic DvP, fractional tokens; smart contracts + oracles.
  • Bond math: P = ΣC/(1+y)t + F/(1+y)n; Excel PV, RATE, PRICE, YIELD, DURATION, MDURATION.
  • %ΔP ≈ −MD·Δy + ½C(Δy)2.

Unit 5 — Companies

  • HDFC Bank: merged with HDFC Ltd 1 Jul 2023; carbon neutral ops by FY2031-32; Parivartan, SLI; RBI curbs 2020–22.
  • Infosys: carbon neutral 2020; ESG Vision 2030; RE100; 2017 governance episode.
  • ITC: carbon, water, waste-recycling positive; e-Choupal 2000; tobacco exclusion.
  • JSW Steel: US$500m SLB 2021 (Vijayanagar CO2 intensity); net zero 2050; CBAM risk.
  • Vedanta: Niyamgiri (SC 2013); Thoothukudi 2018, closure upheld 2024; GPFG exclusion 2007; net zero 2050.

Exam-day checklist

  • Carry calculator (for SLB, PCAF, bond numbers), two pens, pencil and ruler for diagrams.
  • Paper (course plan): Section A 5 × 5; Section B 5 × 10; Section C compulsory 15-mark case. Check the printed duration and any (a)/(b) choices, then give time in proportion to marks (the mid-sem rate was 2.4 minutes per mark).
  • Evaluative questions ("critically evaluate", "is it a paradigm shift") need a clear stance — the mid-sem scheme capped descriptive answers at 7–8 out of 10.
  • Read the case first; underline numbers and sub-questions; answer each sub-part under its own heading.
  • Every answer: definition → headed points → diagram/table → Indian example → conclusion.
  • Name frameworks: ICMA GBP/SLBP, TCFD, ISSB, GRI, BRSR, PCAF, NGFS.
  • Write units: tCO2e, ₹ crore, bps; state formulas before substituting.
  • For company questions use the six-step template: profile, material issues, commitments, evidence, risks, investment view.
  • End every case with a clear recommendation plus conditions.
  • Last 5 minutes: check numbering, units, and that no sub-question is missed.

End of guide. Go back to the start or the coverage tracker for any topic you want to revisit.
